However, before this time dating is less certain. This tablet is one of the most important (and controversial) cuneiform tablets for reconstructing Mesopotamian chronology before around 1400 BC. The text of the tablet is a copy, made at Nineveh in the seventh century BC, of observations of the planet Venus made in the reign of Ammisaduqa, king of Babylon, about 1000 years earlier. Modern astronomers have used the details of the observations in an attempt to calculate the dates of Ammisaduqa (reigned 1646-26 BC). Ideally this process would also allow us to date the Babylonian rulers of the early second and late third millennium BC. Unfortunately, however, there is much uncertainty in the dating because the records are so inconsistent. This has led to different chronologies being adopted with some scholars favouring a \u0027high\u0027 chronology while others adopt a \u0027middle\u0027 or \u0027low\u0027 range of dates. jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md; mousedown:jScpfc; touchstart:jScpfc; touchmove:NI7Syc; mouseup:nHXBQb; touchend:nHXBQb; mouseleave:FfcXAd; blur:ll011b;" jscontroller="V99V9" jsshadow><div class="TgRqnd" jsname="ksKsZd" aria-hidden="true"></div><span jsslot class="WMuzR uMxnXe"><img class="o5lju S3q9Wc" alt="British Museum" data-bgsrc="//lh3.googleusercontent.com/ci/AL18g_Qh4UkBCehl98GW_MyXt1npE7Ov0jQQIEdCdUBG-4cIPIdxjVY-QJvA3Ja8ehkX_Dr9rBJqmKEW=fcrop64=1,00000000ffffffac" /><div class="r5MUSe"><h3 class="To7WBf">British Museum<br><span class="WrfKPd">London, United Kingdom</span></h3></div></span></a></header></div><section class="WDSAyb QwmCXd"><div class="R5VDUc"><p>Thanks to <a href="/entity/m0wmn" data-gacategory="annotation" data-gaaction="clicked" data-galabel="assetpage_injected_link_v1">Assyrian</a> records, the chronology of Mesopotamia is relatively clear back to around 1200 BC. However, before this time dating is less certain. This tablet is one of the most important (and controversial) cuneiform tablets for reconstructing Mesopotamian chronology before around 1400 BC.</p><p>The text of the tablet is a copy, made at Nineveh in the seventh century BC, of observations of the planet Venus made in the reign of Ammisaduqa, king of Babylon, about 1000 years earlier. Modern astronomers have used the details of the observations in an attempt to calculate the dates of Ammisaduqa (reigned 1646-26 BC). Ideally this process would also allow us to date the <a href="/entity/m0cn3b" data-gacategory="annotation" data-gaaction="clicked" data-galabel="assetpage_injected_link_v1">Babylonian</a> rulers of the early second and late third millennium BC. Unfortunately, however, there is much uncertainty in the dating because the records are so inconsistent. This has led to different chronologies being adopted with some scholars favouring a &#39;high&#39; chronology while others adopt a &#39;middle&#39; or &#39;low&#39; range of dates. There are good arguments for each of these.</p></div></section><span class="Wstvoc"></span><section class="rw8Th QwmCXd" jsname="jfdpUb"><p class="ZWm5Kd" jsname="pG32ge">Details</p><div
